### InstaDA: Augmenting Instance Segmentation Data with Dual-Agent System
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.02973v1)
- **Authors**: Xianbao Hou, Yonghao He, Zeyd Boukhers, John See, Hu Su, Wei Sui, Cong Yang
- **Abstract**: Acquiring high-quality instance segmentation data is challenging due to the labor-intensive nature of the annotation process and significant class imbalances within datasets. Recent studies have utilized the integration of Copy-Paste and diffusion models to create more diverse datasets. However, these studies often lack deep collaboration between large language models (LLMs) and diffusion models, and underutilize the rich information within the existing training data. To address these limitations, we propose InstaDA, a novel, training-free Dual-Agent system designed to augment instance segmentation datasets. First, we introduce a Text-Agent (T-Agent) that enhances data diversity through collaboration between LLMs and diffusion models. This agent features a novel Prompt Rethink mechanism, which iteratively refines prompts based on the generated images. This process not only fosters collaboration but also increases image utilization and optimizes the prompts themselves. Additionally, we present an Image-Agent (I-Agent) aimed at enriching the overall data distribution. This agent augments the training set by generating new instances conditioned on the training images. To ensure practicality and efficiency, both agents operate as independent and automated workflows, enhancing usability. Experiments conducted on the LVIS 1.0 validation set indicate that InstaDA achieves significant improvements, with an increase of +4.0 in box average precision (AP) and +3.3 in mask AP compared to the baseline. Furthermore, it outperforms the leading model, DiverGen, by +0.3 in box AP and +0.1 in mask AP, with a notable +0.7 gain in box AP on common categories and mask AP gains of +0.2 on common categories and +0.5 on frequent categories.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces InstaDA, a novel dual-agent system designed to augment instance segmentation datasets without requiring additional annotations. The system consists of two agents: a Text-Agent (T-Agent) that uses LLMs and diffusion models to generate diverse data through a "Prompt Rethink" mechanism, and an Image-Agent (I-Agent) which leverages ControlNet and image-to-image translation to enrich the training data distribution using the existing annotated dataset.  A novel CLIP dual-similarity metric is used to filter generated instances, ensuring high semantic and visual quality.  Experiments on the LVIS dataset demonstrate improvements over a baseline and a state-of-the-art data augmentation method.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty lies in the dual-agent approach and the Prompt Rethink mechanism.  Combining LLMs and diffusion models for data augmentation is not entirely new, but the iterative prompt refinement process and the collaboration between the two agents (T-Agent generating diverse prompts, I-Agent using the existing dataset to create more realistic data) are valuable contributions. The SAM-box strategy for refined annotations and dual-similarity CLIP filtering offer practical enhancements.
    *   Prompt Rethink: The idea of actively refining prompts based on the quality of generated images is clever. It addresses a key limitation of relying solely on LLMs to generate prompts, which can often result in unrealistic or irrelevant outputs.
    *   Dual-Agent system: Augmentation with two-agent system is also a good novelty for instance segmentation area.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from its potential to reduce the reliance on expensive and time-consuming manual annotation for instance segmentation. By generating synthetic data that is both diverse and aligned with the existing dataset, InstaDA could enable more accurate and robust instance segmentation models, particularly in scenarios with limited labeled data or significant class imbalance. The performance gains on LVIS validation set are statistically significant and showcase the effectiveness of the proposed framework. Moreover, the gains in common and frequent categories is a plus. It makes the augmentation approach applicable in datasets in which the imbalance in classes can be overcome by the two-agent approach.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Well-defined and modular framework.
    *   Effective use of LLMs and diffusion models.
    *   The Prompt Rethink mechanism is a strong point.
    *   The integration of SAM-box and CLIP dual-similarity boosts performance.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on LVIS dataset.
    *   The system is training-free, reducing the amount of training time for data generation pipeline.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The dependence on computationally intensive LLMs and diffusion models is still a practical limitation. While the paper addresses this somewhat with LoRA, the computational cost remains considerable for large-scale datasets.
    *   While the paper presents efficiency gains, a more detailed analysis of the wall-clock time and resource consumption would be beneficial.
    *   Further analysis of where the main bottleneck of the proposed framework is.
    *   It could benefit from a discussion of the potential negative biases that could be introduced by the synthetic data, and how to mitigate those. The T-agent can cause the new biases to exist with LLMs are injected.

*   **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence future research in data augmentation for instance segmentation, particularly in exploring more sophisticated ways to leverage LLMs and diffusion models. The Prompt Rethink mechanism could be adopted and extended by other researchers in the field. The automated workflow aspects also offer practical benefits for researchers and practitioners.

Overall, the paper presents a well-designed and effective framework for instance segmentation data augmentation. The Prompt Rethink mechanism and the dual-agent approach are novel contributions that address key limitations of existing methods.

Score: 7.5
Justification: The paper presents significant advancements in the field by making the training data generation pipeline more data-aware, by addressing a number of limitations in previous approaches, especially in the diversity of new samples, and the annotation quality, for the sake of data augmentation for instance segmentation. But, it lacks a deep analysis on the effect of the models used (LLM, Diffusion model) and its possible data bias effect, especially the T-Agent, which generates data in a complete generation. Moreover, some specific limitations and bottlenecks could be explored further to enhance the results with low-cost engineering.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### DiaCBT: A Long-Periodic Dialogue Corpus Guided by Cognitive Conceptualization Diagram for CBT-based Psychological Counseling
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.02999v1)
- **Authors**: Yougen Zhou, Ningning Zhou, Qin Chen, Jie Zhou, Aimin Zhou, Liang He
- **Abstract**: Psychotherapy reaches only a small fraction of individuals suffering from mental disorders due to social stigma and the limited availability of therapists. Large language models (LLMs), when equipped with professional psychotherapeutic skills, offer a promising solution to expand access to mental health services. However, the lack of psychological conversation datasets presents significant challenges in developing effective psychotherapy-guided conversational agents. In this paper, we construct a long-periodic dialogue corpus for counseling based on cognitive behavioral therapy (CBT). Our curated dataset includes multiple sessions for each counseling and incorporates cognitive conceptualization diagrams (CCDs) to guide client simulation across diverse scenarios. To evaluate the utility of our dataset, we train an in-depth counseling model and present a comprehensive evaluation framework to benchmark it against established psychological criteria for CBT-based counseling. Results demonstrate that DiaCBT effectively enhances LLMs' ability to emulate psychologists with CBT expertise, underscoring its potential for training more professional counseling agents.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DiaCBT, a novel, long-periodic dialogue corpus designed to facilitate the training of AI-driven psychological counseling agents based on Cognitive Behavioral Therapy (CBT). Recognizing the limitations of existing psychotherapy datasets (short conversations, lack of multi-session structure, and insufficient client modeling), DiaCBT incorporates Cognitive Conceptualization Diagrams (CCDs) to guide client simulations across diverse scenarios. The authors curate a dataset annotated with CBT strategies and then fine-tune a language model on it. They also propose a comprehensive evaluation framework, benchmarking the model against established psychological criteria. Experimental results demonstrate that DiaCBT enhances the language model's ability to emulate CBT-proficient psychologists, suggesting its value for training more professional counseling agents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the construction of a long-periodic CBT-based counseling dialogue dataset.  Existing datasets are often limited in length, depth, and realism. The incorporation of CCDs to guide client modeling represents a significant step forward.  While there have been efforts to synthesize dialogues before, DiaCBT differentiates itself through its multi-session structure and detailed CBT annotation. The rigorous evaluation framework is also a strong point. However, using LLMs for synthetic data generation in the mental health domain is an emerging trend, and the core techniques used in this study (fine-tuning and prompting LLMs) are relatively standard.

**Significance:** The development of effective AI-based mental health support tools is crucial for addressing the growing mental health crisis.  DiaCBT has the potential to contribute to this field by providing a valuable resource for training and evaluating conversational agents. The comprehensive evaluation framework provides a benchmark for future research.  However, the impact of this research hinges on the quality and realism of the generated dialogues and the ability to generalize findings to real-world clinical settings.  The ethical considerations surrounding the deployment of AI-based mental health tools also need careful consideration.

**Strengths:**

*   **High-quality dataset construction:** The integration of CCDs and CBT strategies enhances the realism and relevance of the dialogues.
*   **Comprehensive evaluation framework:** The use of both automatic and human evaluations, along with established psychological criteria, provides a robust assessment of the model's performance.
*   **Clear presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the methodology and results.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Synthetic Data:** Reliance on synthetic data, while addressing privacy concerns, raises questions about realism. Are the simulated clients truly representative of the spectrum of mental health patients and experiences? Validation against real-world clinical data would strengthen the study.
*   **Limited Novelty of Model Training:** The training of the model used is fairly standard and well-established, involving fine-tuning.  The real innovation lies in the data itself.
*   **Generalizability:** Although CCD provides some guidance, it is not clear how well the model would generalize to scenarios outside of the dataset's scope.
*   **Limited scope of CBT:** While the dataset contains multiple CBT techniques, certain psychological conditions are excluded.

**Justification for Score:**

While DiaCBT presents a valuable and comprehensive resource for advancing AI-driven mental health support, the study has some limitations. The novelty is moderate since the techniques (fine-tuning of language models) are not particularly cutting-edge, but the dataset is a valuable contribution.  The quality of the dialogue and potential real-world impact require further validation.  Also, it might be challenging to scale this effort to additional psychotherapeutic orientations. Given its solid methodology, potential influence, and identified areas for future improvement, this paper merits a score of:

### Enhancing Robustness in Post-Processing Watermarking: An Ensemble Attack Network Using CNNs and Transformers
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03006v1)
- **Authors**: Tzuhsuan Huang, Cheng Yu Yeo, Tsai-Ling Huang, Hong-Han Shuai, Wen-Huang Cheng, Jun-Cheng Chen
- **Abstract**: Recent studies on deep watermarking have predominantly focused on in-processing watermarking, which integrates the watermarking process into image generation. However, post-processing watermarking, which embeds watermarks after image generation, offers more flexibility. It can be applied to outputs from any generative model (e.g. GANs, diffusion models) without needing access to the model's internal structure. It also allows users to embed unique watermarks into individual images. Therefore, this study focuses on post-processing watermarking and enhances its robustness by incorporating an ensemble attack network during training. We construct various versions of attack networks using CNN and Transformer in both spatial and frequency domains to investigate how each combination influences the robustness of the watermarking model. Our results demonstrate that combining a CNN-based attack network in the spatial domain with a Transformer-based attack network in the frequency domain yields the highest robustness in watermarking models. Extensive evaluation on the WAVES benchmark, using average bit accuracy as the metric, demonstrates that our ensemble attack network significantly enhances the robustness of baseline watermarking methods under various stress tests. In particular, for the Regeneration Attack defined in WAVES, our method improves StegaStamp by 18.743%. The code is released at:https://github.com/aiiu-lab/DeepRobustWatermark.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of improving the robustness of post-processing deep watermarking techniques.  The core idea is to enhance the adversarial training process by employing an ensemble attack network composed of CNNs and Transformers operating in both spatial and frequency domains. The paper argues that CNN-based attacks, used in previous work, are limited in their ability to simulate complex distortions (e.g., JPEG compression), while Transformer-based attacks can cause noticeable artifacts. The proposed ensemble approach aims to combine the strengths of both, leading to improved robustness against a wider range of attacks. The paper evaluates the proposed method extensively using the WAVES benchmark, showing improvements over baseline watermarking methods like HiDDeN and StegaStamp. The experiments also include manipulating watermarked images with image editing models.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The novelty of the paper lies primarily in the *ensemble approach* to constructing the attack network for adversarial training in post-processing watermarking. While CNN-based attack networks and the use of Transformers in image processing are known, the specific combination of CNNs and Transformers in spatial and frequency domains for adversarial training in this context is a significant contribution. Introducing DCT-Transformer is another novel idea to preserve image quality, but the benefits are not as significant as combining it with CNN-based attacks.
* **Significance:** The significance of this work is that it provides a more effective approach to train robust post-processing watermarking models. By employing an ensemble attack network that generates a diverse range of distortions, the watermarking model becomes more resilient against a broader spectrum of attacks than previous methods.  The demonstrated improvements on the WAVES benchmark and against image editing models clearly showcases the effectiveness of the ensemble approach. The work directly tackles the practical limitations of deploying deep watermarking methods in real-world scenarios where images are likely to undergo various processing and distortion.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper features extensive experiments and analysis, including using the WAVES benchmark and image editing models, which provides strong empirical evidence for the effectiveness of the proposed method.
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the motivation, methodology, and results.  Figures are used effectively to illustrate the proposed approach and the experimental setup.
    * **Addresses a Practical Problem:** Improving robustness against various attacks is critical for the adoption of watermarking in real-world applications.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Complexity:** The proposed ensemble approach adds complexity to the training process, which might increase computational cost compared to simpler attack networks.
    * **Perceptual Quality Trade-off:** The paper acknowledges a trade-off between robustness and perceptual quality, with the ensemble approach leading to slightly lower PSNR and SSIM scores than some baseline methods. While the images are still visually acceptable, further work might be needed to address this trade-off.
    * **Limited Scope of Comparison:** The baselines considered are HiDDeN, StegaStamp and DA. Expanding the comparison to more recent and advanced watermarking schemes could further strengthen the claim of state-of-the-art performance.

* **Potential Influence:** This paper has the potential to influence future research in deep watermarking by demonstrating the effectiveness of ensemble attack networks for adversarial training. Other researchers can build upon this work by exploring different ensemble configurations, alternative network architectures, or novel frequency-domain techniques. The findings could also be relevant to other security applications where adversarial training is used to improve robustness.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of deep watermarking. The ensemble attack network is a practical and effective approach to improving the robustness of watermarking models against a diverse range of attacks.  While there are some limitations regarding complexity and perceptual quality, the strengths of the paper outweigh the weaknesses.  The comprehensive evaluation and clear presentation make this work a valuable contribution to the field. The work can motivate other researchers to explore novel attack networks.

### Training LLMs to be Better Text Embedders through Bidirectional Reconstruction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03020v1)
- **Authors**: Chang Su, Dengliang Shi, Siyuan Huang, Jintao Du, Changhua Meng, Yu Cheng, Weiqiang Wang, Zhouhan Lin
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have increasingly been explored as powerful text embedders. Existing LLM-based text embedding approaches often leverage the embedding of the final token, typically a reserved special token such as [EOS]. However, these tokens have not been intentionally trained to capture the semantics of the whole context, limiting their capacity as text embeddings, especially for retrieval and re-ranking tasks. We propose to add a new training stage before contrastive learning to enrich the semantics of the final token embedding. This stage employs bidirectional generative reconstruction tasks, namely EBQ2D (Embedding-Based Query-to-Document) and EBD2Q (Embedding-Based Document-to-Query), which interleave to anchor the [EOS] embedding and reconstruct either side of Query-Document pairs. Experimental results demonstrate that our additional training stage significantly improves LLM performance on the Massive Text Embedding Benchmark (MTEB), achieving new state-of-the-art results across different LLM base models and scales.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Training LLMs to be Better Text Embedders through Bidirectional Reconstruction" addresses the sub-optimal use of the final token embedding (e.g., [EOS]) in Large Language Models (LLMs) when employed for text embedding tasks.  Existing LLM-based embeddings often leverage these tokens, but they aren't specifically trained to capture the comprehensive semantic information of the entire input text. To overcome this limitation, the authors propose a two-stage training framework.  The first stage introduces two novel bidirectional generative reconstruction tasks: Embedding-Based Query-to-Document (EBQ2D) and Embedding-Based Document-to-Query (EBD2Q). These tasks aim to anchor the [EOS] embedding to aggregate semantic information and serve as a reference for generating the counterpart text (either query or document). The second stage employs contrastive learning to further refine the embeddings.  The authors apply their framework to several decoder-only LLMs and demonstrate significant performance improvements on the Massive Text Embedding Benchmark (MTEB), achieving state-of-the-art results compared to models trained solely on publicly available data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the introduction of the bidirectional reconstruction pre-training stage (EBQ2D and EBD2Q) designed to enhance the semantic anchoring of the [EOS] token specifically for embedding tasks. While auto-reconstruction objectives have been explored before, their adaptation to the specific problem of aligning LLM embeddings for retrieval, especially by conditioning generation on the final token embedding in both directions, is a significant contribution. The paper's insight into the mismatch between the traditional role of [EOS] and its function in embedding tasks is also novel. The application of these tasks before contrastive learning refines the final token.

**Significance:** The paper's significance stems from the demonstrated improvements in LLM-based text embedding performance.  Achieving state-of-the-art results on MTEB, a widely recognized benchmark, with publicly available data validates the effectiveness of the proposed approach.  The approach is relatively simple and doesn't require architectural modifications to the LLM, increasing its practicality.  The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of each training stage (bidirectional reconstruction and contrastive learning). These findings show the importance of encoding semantic alignment into the [EOS] representation, which can improve downstream task performance. A potential downside is that this pretraining approach seems tailored toward retrieval-style tasks more than others, and adding retrieval tasks in the second stage is potentially problematic.

**Strengths:**
*   Clear problem definition: The paper clearly identifies a specific problem (sub-optimal [EOS] embedding for text embedding tasks).
*   Novel solution: The bidirectional reconstruction approach is a novel and well-motivated solution.
*   Strong experimental results: The paper provides compelling experimental results demonstrating the effectiveness of the approach on MTEB, including state-of-the-art performance.
*   Ablation studies:  The ablation studies help understand the contribution of each component of the framework.
*   Practical: The approach doesn't require modifying the LLM architecture.

**Weaknesses:**
*   Limited task diversity: The evaluation primarily focuses on MTEB, which has a strong emphasis on retrieval-related tasks. Further evaluation on a broader range of embedding tasks (e.g., classification, clustering with greater task diversity) would strengthen the paper.
*   Relatively small models: The improvement from the authors is that they use two stages of pre-training before fine-tuning, however this is only tested on relatively small models.
*   Lack of theoretical analysis: A theoretical analysis or deeper exploration into why the bidirectional reconstruction is effective, e.g., through probing or visualization, would enhance the paper.

**Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field by providing a simple yet effective method for improving LLM-based text embeddings. The insights into the role of the [EOS] token could lead to further research on developing more targeted training strategies for LLMs used as text encoders.  The easy implementation of the approach and strong experimental results make it likely that other researchers and practitioners will adopt and build upon this work. The approach requires a particular structure for the data, which limits its generalizability.

**Rigorous Rationale:** The paper tackles a specific problem in LLM-based text embeddings with a novel and well-motivated solution, yielding strong empirical results on MTEB. The limitations regarding task diversity in the evaluation and lack of theoretical analysis are valid concerns, but the overall contribution to the field warrants a high score. However, the novelty isn't revolutionary; the basic building blocks aren't new.

### A Study on Zero-Shot Non-Intrusive Speech Intelligibility for Hearing Aids Using Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03021v1)
- **Authors**: Ryandhimas E. Zezario, Dyah A. M. G. Wisnu, Hsin-Min Wang, Yu Tsao
- **Abstract**: This work focuses on zero-shot non-intrusive speech assessment for hearing aids (HA) using large language models (LLMs). Specifically, we introduce GPT-Whisper-HA, an extension of GPT-Whisper, a zero-shot non-intrusive speech assessment model based on LLMs. GPT-Whisper-HA is designed for speech assessment for HA, incorporating MSBG hearing loss and NAL-R simulations to process audio input based on each individual's audiogram, two automatic speech recognition (ASR) modules for audio-to-text representation, and GPT-4o to predict two corresponding scores, followed by score averaging for the final estimated score. Experimental results indicate that GPT-Whisper-HA achieves a 2.59% relative root mean square error (RMSE) improvement over GPT-Whisper, confirming the potential of LLMs for zero-shot speech assessment in predicting subjective intelligibility for HA users.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GPT-Whisper-HA, an extension of the GPT-Whisper model, designed for zero-shot non-intrusive speech intelligibility assessment for hearing aids (HAs). GPT-Whisper-HA incorporates MSBG hearing loss and NAL-R simulations to mimic the audio processing of hearing aids based on individual audiograms. It uses two Automatic Speech Recognition (ASR) modules (small and large Whisper variants) to generate text transcriptions, and GPT-40 to predict intelligibility scores based on the naturalness of the generated text. These scores are then averaged to produce a final intelligibility assessment. The paper shows experimental results on the Clarity Prediction Challenge (CPC) 2023 dataset, demonstrating a modest improvement over the original GPT-Whisper model.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper addresses a practical and important problem: the objective assessment of speech intelligibility for hearing aid users, which is traditionally a labor-intensive and subjective process.
    *   The use of Large Language Models (LLMs) in a zero-shot setting is a promising approach, as it reduces the need for extensive training data, a major bottleneck in this field.
    *   The integration of hearing aid-specific simulations (MSBG and NAL-R) is a logical and relevant modification for the target application, making the model more attuned to the nuances of hearing-impaired speech.
    *   The use of two ASR modules as a sort of "sanity check" provides a degree of robustness to the system.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The improvement over the original GPT-Whisper is relatively small (2.59% relative RMSE improvement). While positive, it suggests that the proposed modifications may not be drastically impactful. This could be interpreted as an incremental rather than groundbreaking advance.
    *   The performance is still significantly lower than the supervised models (MBI-Net/MBI-Net+), indicating that zero-shot learning is not yet competitive with traditional supervised methods. The gap is substantial.
    *   The paper relies heavily on existing technologies (GPT-Whisper, GPT-40, Whisper ASR). The novelty is primarily in the combination of these elements and the adaptation to the hearing aid domain. The individual components are not novel.
    *   The evaluation is limited to a single dataset (CPC 2023). Wider evaluation on other datasets and conditions is needed to ensure generalizability.

*   **Novelty and Significance:**

    *   The main contribution is the adaptation of a zero-shot LLM-based speech intelligibility assessment to the specific context of hearing aids. While this is a relevant adaptation, it doesn't introduce fundamentally new concepts or methodologies. The novelty is primarily in the application of existing tools to a particular problem.
    *   The performance improvement, though present, is relatively small. This weakens the case for the practical significance of the work.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a worthwhile contribution to the field of speech intelligibility assessment for hearing aids. However, the incremental nature of the improvement and the dependence on existing technologies limit its overall impact. While the approach is interesting and potentially scalable, it's not a breakthrough innovation. The modifications are logical and tailored for hearing aids, but the relative performance improvement suggests that the LLMs may require further adaptation to handle hearing-impaired speech effectively, or that other components of the system contribute more.

### Knowledge Integration for Physics-informed Symbolic Regression Using Pre-trained Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03036v1)
- **Authors**: Bilge Taskin, Wenxiong Xie, Teddy Lazebnik
- **Abstract**: Symbolic regression (SR) has emerged as a powerful tool for automated scientific discovery, enabling the derivation of governing equations from experimental data. A growing body of work illustrates the promise of integrating domain knowledge into the SR to improve the discovered equation's generality and usefulness. Physics-informed SR (PiSR) addresses this by incorporating domain knowledge, but current methods often require specialized formulations and manual feature engineering, limiting their adaptability only to domain experts. In this study, we leverage pre-trained Large Language Models (LLMs) to facilitate knowledge integration in PiSR. By harnessing the contextual understanding of LLMs trained on vast scientific literature, we aim to automate the incorporation of domain knowledge, reducing the need for manual intervention and making the process more accessible to a broader range of scientific problems. Namely, the LLM is integrated into the SR's loss function, adding a term of the LLM's evaluation of the SR's produced equation. We extensively evaluate our method using three SR algorithms (DEAP, gplearn, and PySR) and three pre-trained LLMs (Falcon, Mistral, and LLama 2) across three physical dynamics (dropping ball, simple harmonic motion, and electromagnetic wave). The results demonstrate that LLM integration consistently improves the reconstruction of physical dynamics from data, enhancing the robustness of SR models to noise and complexity. We further explore the impact of prompt engineering, finding that more informative prompts significantly improve performance.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the provided paper.

**Summary**

The paper explores using pre-trained Large Language Models (LLMs) to improve physics-informed symbolic regression (PiSR). PiSR aims to discover governing equations from data by incorporating domain knowledge. The authors propose integrating an LLM into the SR's loss function. The LLM evaluates the generated equation based on dimensional consistency, scientific validity, and domain knowledge.  The combined loss guides the SR algorithm. The authors tested the method using three SR algorithms (DEAP, gplearn, PySR), three LLMs (Falcon, Mistral, Llama 2), and three physical dynamics scenarios (free fall, simple harmonic motion, electromagnetic waves). Experiments include benchmarking against SR without LLM, testing different prompt designs, and evaluating robustness against noise. Results indicate that LLM integration improves the reconstruction of physical dynamics, enhances robustness to noise and complexity, and informative prompts improve performance.

**Critical Evaluation**

*Novelty and Significance:*

The core idea of integrating LLMs to guide symbolic regression is not entirely new. Previous works have used LLMs *before* or *after* SR to improve equation generation.  What this paper attempts to do differently is integrate the LLM evaluation *directly into the SR loss function itself*. The authors frame it as a knowledge integration within the search process, differentiating it from pipeline-based approaches. This could potentially allow for a more nuanced feedback loop. That said, the specific *implementation* using a simplistic scoring mechanism derived from LLM's output is arguably incremental. Prior work does include similar scoring components, but not directly integrated into SR.

The significance of the work relies on whether this direct integration can demonstrably improve the SR process in a substantial way. The experiments presented offer preliminary support for this, but also some concerns (below).  If the direct integration can consistently lead to more physically meaningful equations with better generalization properties than the existing pipeline-based approaches or standard PiSR, then it represents a meaningful step forward.

*Strengths:*

*   **Clear Problem Statement:** The paper addresses a relevant problem: how to effectively incorporate domain knowledge into SR to improve its results and make it more accessible.
*   **Systematic Evaluation:** The experiments are well-structured, considering multiple SR algorithms, LLMs, and physical scenarios.  The prompt design experimentation is also a positive aspect.
*   **Promising Results:** The results suggest that the LLM integration does, in fact, improve the SR process in terms of reconstruction accuracy, noise robustness and alignment with GT equation.
*   **Addresses a Gap:** The paper clearly identifies a limitation of existing PiSR methods (reliance on manual feature engineering) and proposes a way to automate knowledge integration.

*Weaknesses:*

*   **Limited LLM Interaction:**  The LLM interaction is simplistic.  The prompt engineering is limited to providing context and a desired output format, with a very simplified scoring schema. The LLM doesn't engage in a more complex reasoning process. A three-element score (dimensional consistency, simplicity, realism) seems a rather blunt assessment. The use of a zero-temperature configuration, although understandable for consistent evaluation, further reduces the LLM's potential reasoning abilities. Could the LLM provide gradients? Could it suggest relevant transformations? This integration, while direct, is shallow.
*   **Overly Specific Prompts:** The approach is highly prompt-dependent. Though the prompt engineering experiment is worthwhile, it also highlights the vulnerability. This makes the approach sensitive, potentially lacking generalizability. The success relies heavily on crafting an "ideal" prompt.  The effectiveness of a prompt cannot easily be guaranteed.
*   **Lack of Theoretical Justification:** The paper lacks a strong theoretical justification for why this particular integration method is optimal or preferred. Why a simple scoring system rather than a more sophisticated interaction?
*   **Synthetically Generated Data:** Testing on synthetic data is necessary for controlled experimentation, but it can also be a limitation. The performance on real-world data, with its inherent complexities, needs to be considered.
*   **Comparisons to Existing Approaches:** The comparison to existing PiSR is weak.  The paper mentions that PiSR requires manual feature engineering, but does not compare directly to existing PiSR *methods* on same data. The authors have focused on LLM integration rather than improving SR itself.
*   **Small LLM Size:** The choice of 7B parameter LLMs may have limited their ability to perform the type of complex reasoning desired.
*   **Limited scope**: The physical scenarios considered are relatively simple. The paper needs to demonstrate the effectiveness of this technique in a wide variety of different physics systems to establish its utility.

*Overall Assessment:*

The paper presents an interesting idea with promising, albeit preliminary, results. The direct integration of LLM into SR loss function is a novel approach, but its current implementation is simplistic and relies heavily on prompt engineering. The experiments demonstrate some improvements in performance, but there are concerns regarding the lack of a strong theoretical foundation, limited LLM interaction, and limited scope. The paper needs more rigorous comparisons with existing PiSR approaches, as well as more extensive testing on real-world data. Further research into the interaction design between LLM and SR search, including gradients, more reasoning capabilities, could bring this line of research to the next level.

Score: 5

*Rationale:*
The paper introduces a reasonably novel approach (direct LLM integration in the loss), but the technical execution is incremental and doesn't yet demonstrate a major impact on the field. While the results are positive, several limitations hinder its overall significance and novelty. These limitations, and lack of a strong validation against the state of the art lead to a neutral score, rather than positive.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 5/10

### MedLiteNet: Lightweight Hybrid Medical Image Segmentation Model
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03041v1)
- **Authors**: Pengyang Yu, Haoquan Wang, Gerard Marks, Tahar Kechadi, Laurence T. Yang, Sahraoui Dhelim, Nyothiri Aung
- **Abstract**: Accurate skin-lesion segmentation remains a key technical challenge for computer-aided diagnosis of skin cancer. Convolutional neural networks, while effective, are constrained by limited receptive fields and thus struggle to model long-range dependencies. Vision Transformers capture global context, yet their quadratic complexity and large parameter budgets hinder use on the small-sample medical datasets common in dermatology. We introduce the MedLiteNet, a lightweight CNN Transformer hybrid tailored for dermoscopic segmentation that achieves high precision through hierarchical feature extraction and multi-scale context aggregation. The encoder stacks depth-wise Mobile Inverted Bottleneck blocks to curb computation, inserts a bottleneck-level cross-scale token-mixing unit to exchange information between resolutions, and embeds a boundary-aware self-attention module to sharpen lesion contours.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "MedLiteNet: Lightweight Hybrid Medical Image Segmentation Model" introduces a novel CNN-Transformer hybrid architecture designed for accurate and efficient skin lesion segmentation in dermoscopic images. The core idea is to create a lightweight model suitable for resource-constrained environments (e.g., mobile devices) while maintaining high segmentation accuracy. The MedLiteNet uses a depth-wise separable Mobile Inverted Bottleneck (MBConv) encoder, a cross-scale Local-Global Block for fusing local and global features, a boundary-aware self-attention module, and an ASPP (Atrous Spatial Pyramid Pooling) decoder with SCSE (Spatial-Channel Squeeze-and-Excitation) attention. The model achieves competitive performance on the ISIC 2018 dataset with significantly fewer parameters compared to existing CNN-Transformer hybrids, making it suitable for real-time or mobile deployment.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty lies in the specific architecture design and the focus on extreme lightweighting. While CNN-Transformer hybrids are not entirely new, MedLiteNet distinguishes itself through its careful component selection (MBConv, efficient attention mechanism, specialized loss function) and integration aimed at minimizing the parameter count without significantly sacrificing accuracy. The boundary-aware self-attention is a good touch. The design of the "local-global-boundary" triple-coupling architecture seems well crafted for the task. The optimization for FP16 inference and size-increment training also contributes to the practical aspect.

*   **Significance:** The significance is in addressing the crucial challenge of deploying medical image segmentation models in resource-constrained settings. Many existing high-accuracy models are too computationally expensive for mobile devices or point-of-care applications. By achieving competitive performance with a very small footprint, MedLiteNet opens up possibilities for wider adoption of AI-assisted diagnostics in dermatology. This could potentially improve early detection and treatment of skin cancer, especially in areas with limited access to specialized medical resources.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Lightweight Design:** The primary strength is its small parameter count and low computational cost, as demonstrated by the fast inference time.
    *   **Competitive Performance:** It achieves a good balance between accuracy and efficiency, closely approaching the performance of larger models.
    *   **Clear Architecture:** The paper provides a clear description of the model architecture and the rationale behind each component.
    *   **Addressing a Practical Problem:** It targets a very relevant and important problem in medical image analysis.
    *   **Strong evaluation on a well-established dataset:** The ISIC 2018 dataset is a good benchmark.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Improvement:**  While the results are good, the improvement over existing lightweight approaches isn't revolutionary. The paper claims "almost the same" performance as the best methods, which might be an overstatement.
    *   **Qualitative Results:**  The qualitative results show accurate boundary delineation for typical lesions. However, there's room for more extensive discussion of failure cases and how the model handles challenging situations.
    *   **Limited Ablation Studies:**  Ablation studies exploring the contribution of each component (e.g., boundary-aware attention, cross-scale fusion) could strengthen the paper further. While the architecture seems well-crafted, ablation experiments would empirically demonstrate the importance of each element.
    *   **Lack of Comparison to SOTA lightweight models:**  The paper could have benefited from a more direct comparison against other recently published, specifically optimized, lightweight medical image segmentation methods, if available.

*   **Potential Influence:** MedLiteNet's design principles and successful implementation could inspire future research on resource-efficient medical image analysis. The attention to mobile deployment is forward-thinking. If the code is made available, it will likely be adopted and extended by other researchers. The practical focus on latency is extremely welcome, especially in medical applications.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **7**.  The paper demonstrates a solid contribution to the field by introducing a very lightweight and reasonably accurate skin lesion segmentation model. The architecture is well-motivated, and the experimental results support the claims. However, the gains over existing methods are incremental rather than transformative. Further analysis of failure cases, more rigorous ablation studies, and a stronger comparative analysis against state-of-the-art *lightweight* models would have significantly increased the score. The practical focus and potential for deployment are strong positives.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### DCDB: Dynamic Conditional Dual Diffusion Bridge for Ill-posed Multi-Tasks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03044v1)
- **Authors**: Chengjie Huang, Jiafeng Yan, Jing Li, Lu Bai
- **Abstract**: Conditional diffusion models have made impressive progress in the field of image processing, but the characteristics of constructing data distribution pathways make it difficult to exploit the intrinsic correlation between tasks in multi-task scenarios, which is even worse in ill-posed tasks with a lack of training data. In addition, traditional static condition control makes it difficult for networks to learn in multi-task scenarios with its dynamically evolving characteristics. To address these challenges, we propose a dynamic conditional double diffusion bridge training paradigm to build a general framework for ill-posed multi-tasks. Firstly, this paradigm decouples the diffusion and condition generation processes, avoiding the dependence of the diffusion model on supervised data in ill-posed tasks. Secondly, generated by the same noise schedule, dynamic conditions are used to gradually adjust their statistical characteristics, naturally embed time-related information, and reduce the difficulty of network learning. We analyze the learning objectives of the network under different conditional forms in the single-step denoising process and compare the changes in its attention weights in the network, demonstrating the superiority of our dynamic conditions. Taking dehazing and visible-infrared fusion as typical ill-posed multi-task scenarios, we achieve the best performance in multiple indicators on public datasets. The code has been publicly released at: https://anonymous.4open.science/r/DCDB-D3C2.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a Dynamic Conditional Dual Diffusion Bridge (DCDB) training paradigm for ill-posed multi-task image processing problems.  It addresses limitations in conditional diffusion models, which struggle with ill-posed tasks (lacking ground truth) and fail to effectively leverage task correlations. DCDB decouples diffusion and condition generation using two noise-schedule-shared diffusion bridges: a main diffusion bridge (X-bridge) and a conditional generation bridge (Y-bridge).  The Y-bridge generates dynamic conditional signals, progressively adjusting statistical characteristics, reducing Wasserstein distance between conditional distributions, and embedding time-step information. The method is applied to dehazing and visible-infrared fusion, demonstrating improved performance on public datasets.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the dynamic conditional generation using a separate diffusion bridge (Y-bridge) to address the lack of ground truth data and dynamic evolutionary characteristics of ill-posed multi-task problems. Decoupling the diffusion process from the conditional generation and using shared noise schedules also adds novelty. The analysis of attention weights provides insights. However, diffusion bridges themselves are not a completely novel concept, and much of the U-net arch is very standard.
*   **Significance:** The significance is in providing a more effective and general framework for handling ill-posed multi-task image processing. The improved performance on the challenging tasks of dehazing and visible-infrared fusion suggests practical value. Overcoming the dependence on ground truth data opens doors to applications where paired data is unavailable or difficult to acquire. By reducing the Wasserstein distance and incorporating time information, it facilitates more efficient training, and has the potential to be used in many areas.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Problem Addressed:** The paper effectively addresses the limitations of current conditional diffusion models for ill-posed multi-task scenarios.
    *   **Technical Approach:** The DCDB paradigm and dynamic conditional generation show promise in handling these challenges.
    *   **Empirical Results:** Strong experimental results on dehazing and visible-infrared fusion support the effectiveness of the approach.
    *   **Theoretical Justification:** The mathematical justification and analysis of attention weights provide insights into the method's workings.
    *   **Code Availability:** The public release of code is an excellent way of improving reproducibility.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Generality:**  While applied to dehazing and fusion, the broader applicability to other ill-posed multi-task problems needs further exploration.
    *   **Complexity:** The two diffusion bridge setup can make the network hard to tune, and is more complex than traditional single diffusion paradigms.
    *   **Limited Scenarios:**The approach is limited to conditional control and relies on linear Gaussian dynamics for conditional generation, which might not suit all tasks.
    *   **Lack of Comparison in Ablation:** The paper does not explain the different implementation details in the deblur+fusion comparison, which makes it very difficult to give accurate reasoning to that experiment.
    *   **Related work:** Related work is omitted. How does dynamic conditional generation compare to approaches which use an auxiliary SABW block to generate dynamic data? Also, how does the DCDB compare to multitask learning with diffusion models for hyperspectral imaging (Qu et al, 2024)?

*   **Potential Impact:** The DCDB paradigm has the potential to influence research on multi-task image processing, particularly in areas where ground truth data is scarce. It could also inspire new approaches for dynamically adjusting conditional signals in diffusion models.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper introduces a technically sound and empirically validated approach to a significant problem in image processing. The dynamic conditional generation mechanism is a novel and promising contribution. However, its limitations in scope, the need for further investigation of its generality, and not being compared with approaches with auxiliary data limits its impact. On top of this, limited explanation of the Ablation Experiment and omitted related work leads to some points off.

Score: 6

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### FlashRecovery: Fast and Low-Cost Recovery from Failures for Large-Scale Training of LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03047v1)
- **Authors**: Haijun Zhang, Jinxiang Wang, Zhenhua Yu, Yanyong Zhang, Xuejie Ji, Kaining Mao, Jun Zhang, Yaqing Zhang, Ting Wu, Fei Jie, Xiemin Huang, Zhifang Cai, Junhua Cheng, Shuwei Wang, Wei Li, Xiaoming Bao, Hua Xu, Shixiong Zhao, Jun Li, Hongwei Sun, Ziyang Zhang, Yi Xiong, Chunsheng Li
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have made a profound impact across various fields due to their advanced capabilities. However, training these models at unprecedented scales requires extensive AI accelerator clusters and sophisticated parallelism strategies, which pose significant challenges in maintaining system reliability over prolonged training periods. A major concern is the substantial loss of training time caused by inevitable hardware and software failures. To address these challenges, we present FlashRecovery, a fast and low-cost failure recovery system comprising three core modules: (1) Active and real-time failure detection. This module performs continuous training state monitoring, enabling immediate identification of hardware and software failures within seconds, thus ensuring rapid incident response; (2) Scale-independent task restart. By employing different recovery strategies for normal and faulty nodes, combined with an optimized communication group reconstruction protocol, our approach ensures that the recovery time remains nearly constant, regardless of cluster scale; (3) Checkpoint-free recovery within one step. Our novel recovery mechanism enables single-step restoration, completely eliminating dependence on traditional checkpointing methods and their associated overhead. Collectively, these innovations enable FlashRecovery to achieve optimal Recovery Time Objective (RTO) and Recovery Point Objective (RPO), substantially improving the reliability and efficiency of long-duration LLM training. Experimental results demonstrate that FlashRecovery system can achieve training restoration on training cluster with 4, 800 devices in 150 seconds. We also verify that the time required for failure recovery is nearly consistent for different scales of training tasks.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the "FlashRecovery: Fast and Low-Cost Recovery from Failures for Large-Scale Training of LLMs" paper, including a justified novelty/significance score.

**Summary:**

The paper presents FlashRecovery, a system designed to mitigate the impact of failures during the training of large language models (LLMs) in large-scale distributed environments. FlashRecovery focuses on achieving a low Recovery Time Objective (RTO) and Recovery Point Objective (RPO) through three core components: (1) Active and real-time failure detection using a heartbeat mechanism coupled with device plugins, (2) Scale-independent task restart achieved by selectively restarting only faulty nodes and optimizing communication group setup, and (3) Checkpoint-free recovery within one step utilizing data parallelism replicas to restore model states. The paper includes theoretical analysis of recovery overhead, and experimental results demonstrate fast and scalable failure recovery on a large computing cluster.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Practical Problem:** The paper addresses a very relevant and pressing problem in the field of LLM training. As model sizes and training times increase, the need for robust failure recovery mechanisms becomes paramount.
    *   **Well-Defined Approach:** FlashRecovery offers a comprehensive approach that considers failure detection, task restart, and data restoration. The breakdown into modular components enhances understandability.
    *   **Scalability Focus:** The emphasis on scale-independent task restart and the efforts to optimize communication group establishment are commendable. The theoretical analysis and experimental validation specifically address the scalability challenges.
    *   **Checkpoint-Free Recovery:** The checkpoint-free recovery mechanism, leveraging data parallelism, is a novel element of the design. Eliminating the I/O overhead associated with traditional checkpointing is a significant advantage.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of FlashRecovery in a large-scale environment (up to 4800 devices). The comparative analysis with a vanilla recovery method highlights the performance gains.
    *   **Detailed Analysis of Failure Types:** The analysis of failure types and their frequencies provides valuable insight into the nature of problems encountered during LLM training.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Novelty in Some Components:** While the integration and checkpoint-free recovery are novel, the heartbeat mechanism for failure detection and the selective task restart have existing solutions and improvements could be more innovative.
    *   **Dependency on Data Parallelism:** The checkpoint-free recovery relies heavily on data parallelism. The paper acknowledges in the limitation section the need for checkpointing still to be implemented in the case of the failure of every node in the DP. The paper could address this with implementing further redundancy strategies beyond simple DP (coding, hybrid redundancy scheme etc.).
    *   **System Complexity:** The solution involves close orchestration among many components which could lead to potential bugs. A more robust automated testing regime may improve its ability to function with different models or system configurations.
    *   **Potential for Optimization:** Although the system achieves a low RTO it does not address RPO. While the loss of 1 step may be acceptable in most configurations it does not make guarantees on correctness from training. A robust strategy of evaluating training correctness post-restoration would dramatically improve results.
    *   **Lack of comparison to other failure tolerance algorithms:** The vanilla baseline does not provide a sufficient benchmark to measure against. Implementing other fault-tolerance algorithms from related work and comparing against them would significantly improve its value.

*   **Significance:**
    *   **Potential to improve training efficiency:** The system has the potential to improve the training efficiency of large language models.
    *   **Useful in industry:** The system has the potential to be applied in industry due to the scale of deployment it is designed for.

**Overall Assessment:**

The paper presents a valuable contribution to the field of LLM training by providing a practical and scalable solution to failure recovery. The emphasis on checkpoint-free recovery and the detailed experimental validation are particularly noteworthy. However, the paper could be improved by focusing more on the limitations in fault tolerance, the complexity and the lack of other algorithms to compare performance to. Despite these weaknesses, the paper addresses a relevant problem and offers a significant advancement in the field.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The score of 7.5 reflects the paper's significant contribution to a relevant problem, its innovative checkpoint-free recovery mechanism, and its detailed empirical validation. However, the aforementioned limitations restrict the overall score. The novelty in some components is incremental rather than groundbreaking. While the integration is clever, the dependency on data parallelism and the omission of comparisons against other fault-tolerance algorithms limits the measured scope, and the complexity and potential performance costs require careful consideration.

- **Classification**: cs.DC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Binary Quantization For LLMs Through Dynamic Grouping
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03054v1)
- **Authors**: Xinzhe Zheng, Zhen-Qun Yang, Haoran Xie, S. Joe Qin, Arlene Chen, Fangzhen Lin
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) have demonstrated remarkable performance across a wide range of Natural Language Processing (NLP) tasks, but require substantial memory and computational resources. Binary quantization, which compresses model weights from 16-bit Brain Float to 1-bit representations in {-1, 1}, offers significant reductions in storage and inference costs. However, such aggressive quantization often leads to notable performance degradation compared to more conservative 4-bit quantization methods. In this research, we propose a novel optimization objective tailored for binary quantization, along with three algorithms designed to realize it effectively. Our method enhances blocked quantization by dynamically identifying optimal unstructured sub-matrices through adaptive grouping strategies. Experimental results demonstrate that our approach achieves an average bit length of just 1.007 bits, while maintaining high model quality. Specifically, our quantized LLaMA 3.2 3B model attains a perplexity of 8.23, remarkably close to the original 7.81, and surpasses previous SOTA BiLLM with a perplexity of only 123.90. Furthermore, our method is competitive with SOTA 4-bit approaches such as GPTQ in both performance and efficiency. The compression process is highly efficient, requiring only 14 seconds to quantize the full LLaMA 3.2 3B weights on a single CPU core, with the entire process completing in under 100 minutes and exhibiting embarrassingly parallel properties. Code - https://github.com/johnnyzheng0636/WGM_bi_quan
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel Dynamic Grouping optimization objective for binary quantization of Large Language Models (LLMs). The approach aims to improve upon existing binary quantization techniques, which often suffer from significant performance degradation compared to 4-bit methods. The core idea is to identify optimal groupings of unstructured sub-matrices within the LLM's weight matrix to minimize quantization loss without relying on computationally intensive Hessian calculations. Three algorithms are proposed: Dynamic Grouping (optimal but slow), Greedy Grouping (faster, approximate), and Windowed Greedy Merging (fastest, approximate). Experiments demonstrate that the Windowed Greedy Merging algorithm achieves a good balance between performance and speed, outperforming state-of-the-art binary quantization methods and achieving competitive results with 4-bit quantization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the Dynamic Grouping optimization objective and the subsequent algorithms. While previous works have explored Hessian-based approaches and blocked quantization, the paper offers a new perspective by focusing on dynamic identification of unstructured sub-matrices based on a predetermined quantization loss. The algorithms themselves, especially the Windowed Greedy Merging, are not entirely novel (greedy approaches are well-established), but their application to the specific problem of binary quantization for LLMs and the specific optimization objective constitutes a contribution. The paper addresses a crucial question that the field hadn't deeply investigated yet: How can alternative grouping strategies improve binary quantization beyond uniform blocking and Hessian-based identification?

**Significance:** The significance of the paper stems from the increasing demand for efficient deployment of LLMs on resource-constrained devices. Binary quantization offers the potential for substantial memory and computational savings, but the associated performance degradation has been a major hurdle. By demonstrating a binary quantization method that achieves competitive performance with 4-bit approaches, this paper offers a viable pathway towards more efficient LLM deployment. The fact that the method is efficient enough to be carried out in under 100 minutes with a single CPU core is notable. The paper showcases how carefully designed optimization objectives can extract more out of existing quantization strategies, reducing performance gap without resorting to computationally intensive approaches. However, while the CPU-based quantization is appealing, the final implementation is limited by simulation and still lacks the necessary customized kernels that can realize the same degree of acceleration.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the limitations of existing binary quantization methods and motivates the need for a new approach.
*   **Novel Optimization Objective:** The Dynamic Grouping optimization objective is well-defined and provides a solid foundation for the proposed algorithms.
*   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The paper presents a thorough experimental evaluation, comparing the proposed methods with state-of-the-art techniques on various datasets and tasks.
*   **Efficient Algorithm:** The Windowed Greedy Merging algorithm offers a practical balance between performance and speed, making it suitable for contemporary LLM architectures.
*   **Easy-to-reproduce**: The experiments can be performed entirely in CPU.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Lack of Hardware Implementation and Kernel Optimization:** The primary weakness is that the method is currently limited to simulation. Without custom hardware or optimized kernels for arbitrary matrix manipulations that are necessary to realize the full potential of dynamic grouping,  the real-world benefits are limited. The paper acknowledges this, but it significantly reduces the impact of the work.
*   **Limited Generalizability of Greedy Approaches:**  While the experiments show promising results, greedy algorithms, by their nature, are not guaranteed to find the global optimum. There's a possibility that the performance gains are specific to the evaluated LLMs and datasets. While the paper demonstrates this is not the case with several LLM structures, a generalization study would strengthen the results.
*   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** While the optimization objective and boundary analysis provide some theoretical insights, a more rigorous analysis of the convergence properties and approximation guarantees of the proposed algorithms would be beneficial.

**Score:** 6.5

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel optimization objective for binary quantization that has significant potential to improve the efficiency of LLM deployment. The proposed Windowed Greedy Merging algorithm offers a practical solution that achieves competitive performance with 4-bit quantization. The main reason the score is not higher is the absence of hardware implementation and the limited optimization kernel that restricts the real-world utility and impact of the current research. While the paper delivers an algorithm that can be implemented with a CPU, the full advantages of binary quantization cannot be realized without adequate kernel implementation. The theoretical guarantees and exploration of better optimization criteria for alternative scenarios are also avenues for future improvement.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 6/10

### Structure-Learnable Adapter Fine-Tuning for Parameter-Efficient Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03057v1)
- **Authors**: Ming Gong, Yingnan Deng, Nia Qi, Yujun Zou, Zhihao Xue, Yun Zi
- **Abstract**: This paper addresses the issues of parameter redundancy, rigid structure, and limited task adaptability in the fine-tuning of large language models. It proposes an adapter-based fine-tuning method built on a structure-learnable mechanism. By introducing differentiable gating functions and structural sparsity control variables, the method enables automatic optimization of adapter insertion points, activation paths, and module combinations. This allows the model to adjust its structure flexibly in multi-task settings to match different task characteristics. With the backbone parameters kept frozen, the method uses a structure search mechanism to guide the dynamic construction of task-specific efficient substructures during training. This significantly improves parameter utilization and representational capacity. In addition, the paper designs a set of sensitivity analysis experiments to systematically evaluate the effects of sparsity weight, noise injection ratio, and data perturbation on model performance. These experiments verify the stability and robustness of the proposed method across various multi-task natural language understanding tasks. The experimental results show that the proposed method outperforms mainstream parameter-efficient tuning techniques on multiple tasks. It achieves a better balance among accuracy, compression rate, and robustness to noise and perturbation.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper based on the provided OCR text.

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a structure-learnable adapter fine-tuning method for large language models (LLMs). Addressing limitations of traditional adapter approaches (fixed topologies, limited task adaptability), the proposed method employs differentiable gating functions and structural sparsity control variables to enable automatic optimization of adapter insertion points, activation paths, and module combinations. This allows the model to dynamically adjust its structure to match different task characteristics while keeping the backbone parameters frozen. A structure search mechanism guides the construction of task-specific, efficient substructures, enhancing parameter utilization and representational capacity. The paper also presents sensitivity analysis experiments to evaluate the effects of sparsity weight, noise injection ratio, and data perturbation on model performance, verifying the method's stability and robustness.  Experimental results demonstrate superior performance compared to mainstream parameter-efficient tuning techniques across multiple natural language understanding tasks, achieving a better balance among accuracy, compression rate, and robustness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The core idea of a structure-learnable adapter is relatively novel, particularly within the specific context of fine-tuning large language models.  The key innovation lies in the automatic optimization of the adapter's topology – specifically, which layers to insert adapters into, how to route information through them, and which modules to activate for different tasks. This differentiates it from previous adapter approaches with manually designed, fixed structures. The sensitivity analysis provides some additional insight into the impact of different hyperparameters on model performance, contributing to a more comprehensive understanding of the proposed method. The novelty of employing differentiable gates for adapter selection is notable, offering a smooth, trainable way to control the contribution of each adapter.

However, the individual components – adapters, sparsity regularization, and gating mechanisms – are not entirely new. The paper combines existing techniques, and the novelty stems primarily from the way these components are integrated and applied to the problem of parameter-efficient LLM fine-tuning.  The connection to neural architecture search (NAS) and dynamic routing is evident, and the work borrows ideas from these fields.

**Significance:**

The work has potential significance for the field of parameter-efficient LLM fine-tuning. Reducing the computational and storage demands associated with fine-tuning massive models is a crucial challenge. The proposed method offers a promising approach by dynamically adjusting the model structure to specific task needs.  The reported improvements in accuracy, compression rate, and robustness (compared to baseline methods like LoRA and Prefix Tuning) are valuable contributions.

However, the significance is somewhat limited by the scope of the experiments and the lack of in-depth analysis. While the performance gains are presented, the paper doesn't deeply explore the learned structures themselves. Understanding *why* certain adapter configurations are preferred for specific tasks would significantly strengthen the contribution. Also, the experiments primarily focus on a specific set of NLU tasks (MT-NLU), limiting the generalizability of the findings.  Expanding the evaluation to a broader range of tasks (e.g., text generation, translation) would be beneficial.

Moreover, the comparison against other efficient fine-tuning techniques, although present, is not exhaustive.  More recent and sophisticated parameter-efficient methods are emerging rapidly, and a more comprehensive comparison would provide a stronger basis for claiming superiority. The discussion of parameter efficiency could be enhanced by explicitly mentioning and comparing floating-point operations per second (FLOPS) or inference speed metrics.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the above analysis, I would assign a score of **6.5**.

*   **Strengths:** The paper presents a relatively novel approach to adapter fine-tuning by incorporating structure learning, leading to improved performance in some NLU tasks. The experiments demonstrate the potential benefits of the method in terms of accuracy, compression, and robustness.
*   **Weaknesses:** The novelty is incremental, building upon existing techniques.  The experimental evaluation is somewhat limited in scope and depth.  The analysis of the learned structures is lacking. There is a need for a broader evaluation and a more robust analysis against state-of-the-art baselines.

The score reflects the potential value of the work, but also acknowledges the limitations in its novelty, scope, and depth of analysis. While the results are promising, further research is needed to fully validate the approach and understand its limitations. The impact on the field will depend on whether the technique can be successfully extended to other tasks and model architectures and whether it can inspire further advancements in structure-learnable fine-tuning.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 6/10

### EverTracer: Hunting Stolen Large Language Models via Stealthy and Robust Probabilistic Fingerprint
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2509.03058v1)
- **Authors**: Zhenhua Xu, Meng Han, Wenpeng Xing
- **Abstract**: The proliferation of large language models (LLMs) has intensified concerns over model theft and license violations, necessitating robust and stealthy ownership verification. Existing fingerprinting methods either require impractical white-box access or introduce detectable statistical anomalies. We propose EverTracer, a novel gray-box fingerprinting framework that ensures stealthy and robust model provenance tracing. EverTracer is the first to repurpose Membership Inference Attacks (MIAs) for defensive use, embedding ownership signals via memorization instead of artificial trigger-output overfitting. It consists of Fingerprint Injection, which fine-tunes the model on any natural language data without detectable artifacts, and Verification, which leverages calibrated probability variation signal to distinguish fingerprinted models. This approach remains robust against adaptive adversaries, including input level modification, and model-level modifications. Extensive experiments across architectures demonstrate EverTracer's state-of-the-art effectiveness, stealthness, and resilience, establishing it as a practical solution for securing LLM intellectual property. Our code and data are publicly available at https://github.com/Xuzhenhua55/EverTracer.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the "EverTracer: Hunting Stolen Large Language Models via Stealthy and Robust Probabilistic Fingerprint" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces EverTracer, a novel gray-box fingerprinting framework for large language models (LLMs) aimed at robust and stealthy ownership verification.  Instead of relying on trigger-output pairs like many existing methods, EverTracer repurposes membership inference attacks (MIAs) to embed ownership signals through memorization of fingerprint data without detectable artifacts. The framework comprises two phases: Fingerprint Injection, where the model is fine-tuned on natural language data (no artificial triggers), and Probability Variation-Based Verification, which uses calibrated probability variations to identify fingerprinted models. This approach claims robustness against adaptive adversaries, including input-level and model-level modifications, while maintaining stealth. Extensive experiments across different LLM architectures are presented to demonstrate EverTracer's effectiveness, stealthiness, and resilience.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of leveraging MIAs defensively to create model fingerprints is a genuinely novel twist.  It departs from the traditional backdoor/trigger-based approach that has dominated the field and offers a fresh perspective on how to embed and detect ownership signals. The use of probability variation for verification, built upon prior work but applied in this specific defensive context, adds to the novelty.
*   **Significance:** Model theft and license violations are significant concerns as LLMs proliferate.  Therefore, a robust and stealthy fingerprinting technique is crucial. If EverTracer truly delivers on its claims of stealth and resilience, it could be a valuable tool for protecting LLM intellectual property. The gray-box nature is important because it aligns with realistic API access constraints, unlike white-box methods.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Stealth:** The avoidance of artificial triggers is a key strength. Perplexity-based filters can easily detect triggers, but EverTracer's approach aims for a more natural fingerprint that blends in.
    *   **Robustness:** The paper presents experimental evidence suggesting robustness against model modifications (pruning, merging, fine-tuning) and input perturbations. This is a significant advantage over many existing methods that are fragile.
    *   **Practicality:** The gray-box constraint and the fact that the method works with natural language data make it potentially more practical for real-world deployment than methods requiring internal model access or special input structures.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Probability Variations:** The core of the verification process relies on subtle probability variations. While the paper attempts to calibrate these variations using a reference model, it's possible that these signals could be sensitive to specific model architectures, training datasets, or optimization techniques. Further, the computational cost and practicality of establishing an accurate baseline with a high-quality reference model are unclear.
    *   **Adaptive Adversary (Erasing Memorized Content):**  The paper acknowledges limitations, including the lack of study of explicit adaptive attacks designed to erase memorized content.  This is a crucial omission because if an attacker can identify and selectively remove the fingerprint data, EverTracer would be defeated. Model unlearning techniques might pose a threat.
    *   **Generality/Transferability:** While the paper demonstrates results across several architectures, it isn't clear how well EverTracer's fingerprints transfer across different LLM families or to models trained on significantly different data. In other words, if a fingerprinted LLaMA model is used to train a new model, could the fingerprint survive that training process?
    *   **Limited Explanation of "Semantically Perturbed Neighborhood":** The quality of the perturbation approach is crucial, and the paper might benefit from a deeper discussion of how effective the T5-Base approach is at creating truly *semantically similar* variants.
    *   **Limited Scope of Adversarial Settings:** While a range of adversarial scenarios are evaluated, the combinations could be more exhaustive. It's possible that the interplay of multiple attack vectors together could weaken the fingerprint. The paper has focused largely on individual dimensions of stealth and robustness.
*   **Justification:**
    *   The paper introduces a novel and compelling technique for addressing a relevant challenge within the field.
